# DEV_CONTEXT.md v0.1

## AI-readable preamble

Dev context is a temporary development-mode working set.

It is created after broad discovery and before implementation. It tells the agent which files are on the desk, which files are supporting references, which risks must be checked, and which sources remain in the archive.

It is not a permanent reviewed knowledge layer and does not replace `MEMORY_TIER.md`.

## 0. Legacy Development Sticky Marker

Legacy marker: this development context/workbench area may still contain old
project-tag or sticky-note language.

From 2026-06-14 onward, do not add new loose "this is useful for VAKS" notes
here for source-level insights.

Use the new route instead:

```text
source-level useful span -> Source Annotation Sidecar
project-level borrow / non-borrow -> Project Overlay
review or action support -> Evidence Packet
```

Existing legacy notes are historical working material. Do not bulk-delete them
until the new annotation workflow is fully landed. When an old sticky note is
touched again, either convert it to `source_annotation`, move project-level scope
into Project Overlay, or mark it as missing source anchor.

## 1. Core Judgment

When the user starts a project, do not load the whole vault into the agent's attention.

Instead:

```text
discover broadly
select relevant materials
build a temporary dev context pack
ask for confirmation to enter development mode
work primarily from the pack
expand only when needed
```

This converts a large knowledge vault into a controlled working memory.

## 2. Dev Context Layers

### Core

Meaning:

```text
Must read and must obey for this task.
```

Use for:

```text
current handoff
current project spec
active architecture decision
active schema or contract
current implementation target
```

Rule:

```text
Core should be small enough to actually load and reason over.
```

Project gate:

```text
Core requires a task, goal, or stop condition.
```

A file is not Core just because it is relevant. It is Core when the current task cannot safely proceed without it.

### Support

Meaning:

```text
Relevant background that can help when design or implementation choices appear.
```

Use for:

```text
reference library reviews
related patterns
prior QA
adjacent implementation notes
similar project examples
```

Rule:

```text
Do not read all support files by default. Expand by need.
```

### Risk

Meaning:

```text
Negative evidence, anti-patterns, failure modes, contradictions, and scope warnings that must not be forgotten.
```

Use for:

```text
anti-patterns
known failure routes
do-not-build-first rules
conflicted claims
wrong-scope warnings
review gates
```

Rule:

```text
Risk is not noise. It is a decision checkpoint.
```

Every high-impact dev context should include at least a minimal risk layer.

PARA does not provide this as a first-class layer.

Keep Risk as a verified-action extension:

```text
PARA tells us where useful material may belong.
Risk tells us what must not be forgotten before action.
```

### Cold References

Meaning:

```text
Original sources and historical records that should remain traceable but not loaded unless evidence is needed.
```

Use for:

```text
raw source files
old query-runs
archived feedback
long source snapshots
external articles or repos
```

Rule:

```text
Keep links and reasons. Open only when tracing evidence, checking history, or resolving conflict.
```

## 3. Development Mode Flow

### Step 1: Discovery

The agent searches the vault for relevant files and signals.

Inputs:

```text
project goal
current task
scope boundaries
known files
known exclusions
```

Output:

```text
candidate context list
```

Candidate discovery signals:

```text
explicit user files
project/domain index pages
scope dashboards / area dashboards
matching scope_key / memory_tier
reviewed relations
source provenance
link overlap
backlinks
tag/category overlap
recent activation
```

Derived relatedness can suggest Support or Cold References.

It cannot make a file Core by itself.

Context briefing:

```text
Before opening full files, produce a compact briefing:
  summaries
  metadata
  memory_tier
  why_suggested
  suggested layer
  source pointers
```

Then choose what to fully read.

This keeps discovery broad without making development mode noisy.

Index-first routing:

```text
If a project or domain index exists, read it before opening individual notes.
Use the index as a map:
  route to likely domains
  inspect summaries/tags
  choose full reads
```

The index is a loading map, not evidence.

### Step 2: Context Pack

The agent creates a temporary context pack using:

```text
Core
Support
Risk
Cold References
Open Questions
Stop Conditions
```

For important or agent-facing packs, also preserve the context envelope:

```text
primary
neighbors
warnings
gaps
suggested_actions
```

Budget trimming may shorten context, but it must not hide critical warnings,
gaps, missing evidence, source-window loss, or action risk. If key support is
trimmed out, the pack cannot remain action-ready.

Retrieval receipts may help assemble a context-pack candidate.

They are not action basis by themselves. A pack that uses adapter output must
still expose source click-back, authority badge, usage badge, warning code, and
stale or scope warnings before it can be reviewed for action-facing use.

Those badges and warnings are preconditions for review only. They are not
enough to create action basis, evidence support, truth confidence, or reviewed
relations. In short: they are not enough to create action basis.

Template:

```text
templates/dev-context-pack.md
```

### Step 3: Confirmation

Before implementation, the agent should report:

```text
Related files are ready.
Core / Support / Risk / Cold References have been separated.
Ask whether to enter development mode.
```

The user can approve, add files, remove files, or ask for a broader scan.

### Step 4: Development

During development:

```text
default to Core
expand to Support when making design choices
check Risk before decisions and writes
open Cold References only for evidence, history, or contradictions
```

### Step 5: Exit

At the end:

```text
write durable outcome back to the vault
record changed assumptions
cool temporary context if no longer active
keep reusable decisions/patterns warm
archive one-off references
```

Project closeout scan:

```text
Core or Support item became reusable:
  write back as warm pattern / decision / implementation note.

Item was only evidence for this task:
  return to cold reference.

Item exposed a failure route:
  write or update Risk / anti-pattern.
```

## 4. Relationship To Base Activation Tier

Base activation tier:

```text
long-term activation hygiene
metadata on notes
lightweight and persistent
```

The historical field name is `memory_tier`, but the concept should be explained as Activation Tier:

```text
The vault has activation tiers, not truth tiers.
Hot means read early, not believe more.
```

Dev context:

```text
short-term project working memory
generated for a task
can include hot, warm, and cold base materials
```

A cold source can be inside a hot dev context as a cold reference.

That means:

```text
it is relevant to the project
but should not be loaded unless evidence is needed
```

PARA mapping for this system:

```text
Projects:
  can inspire Dev Context Core, but only when there is a concrete task/goal.

Areas:
  usually map to Support or durable operating context.

Resources:
  usually map to Support or warm reference material.

Archive:
  usually maps to Cold References.

Risk:
  remains our added layer for anti-patterns, contradictions, and boundaries.
```

This mapping is advisory. It does not change truth status.

Kepano mapping for this system:

```text
categories / Bases:
  can help gather Support candidates and dashboards.

links / backlinks / tag overlap:
  can suggest related context.

templates:
  can keep capture structured without forcing folder movement.

random revisit:
  can create reactivation candidates for old material.
```

These are recall and maintenance signals. They do not replace review.

knowledge-base-server mapping for this system:

```text
kb_context:
  inspires context briefing before full read.

kb_search / kb_search_smart:
  inspires candidate discovery through keyword and semantic retrieval.

SQLite / FTS / embeddings:
  inspire derived retrieval cache, not truth storage.

kb_capture_session / kb_capture_fix:
  inspire closeout writeback and feedback-event candidates.

kb_classify / kb_promote / kb_write:
  useful only behind raw/candidate/review boundaries.
```

These runtime tools help agents find and remember work. They do not replace verified-action promotion.

Dusk mapping for this system:

```text
Area Family / scope dashboard:
  can gather live candidate context: related notes, projects, meetings, tasks, and completed work.

Mail Box / page task:
  can inspire an operational queue for follow-up and closeout.

Monthly note:
  can inspire cooling/reheating review after a work period.

Legacy all-in-one vault:
  warns against making a plugin-heavy full vault the starting architecture.
```

These are workbench and lifecycle ideas. They do not replace the dev context pack or review gate.

## 5. Anti-Drift Rules

Do not let a dev context become stale.

Refresh when:

```text
project goal changes
scope changes
new source invalidates an assumption
implementation moves to a new phase
the user asks for global re-evaluation
```

Do not use a dev context when:

```text
the user asks a general question
the user asks for unrelated exploration
the context pack was built for another project
```

## 6. Minimum Dev Context Pack Fields

```yaml
record_type: dev-context-pack
context_id:
project:
task:
scope_key:
created:
updated:
created_by:
status: active | paused | completed | archived
mode: discovery | development | audit | handoff
core: []
support: []
risk: []
cold_references: []
open_questions: []
stop_conditions: []
refresh_triggers: []
writeback_targets: []
```

## 7. v0.1 Stop Point

This file defines how development-mode working memory should operate.

Not implemented yet:

```text
automatic context pack builder
context freshness scoring
Obsidian Bases view
agent command wrapper
context briefing generator
```

Next concrete step:

```text
Create the first real dev context pack for a project after the user provides the next reference materials.
```

# REVIEW_QUEUE.md v0.1

## AI-readable preamble

This is the first review queue contract for the vault.

The queue is not a dumping ground and not a reviewed knowledge layer. It is where raw sources become reviewable candidate points, and where similar points collide before anything is promoted.

The core alpha mechanism is:

```text
provenance-first
-> candidate point
-> self-contained review candidate record, when review or promotion is intended
-> knowledge calibration / point collision signal
-> reviewed cluster
-> promoted claim / pattern / anti-pattern / decision
```

Knowledge calibration is the practical queue-level version of the vault's truth-maintenance idea:

```text
do not only store a conclusion
record why it is believed
surface what new candidate points support, challenge, narrow, or make stale
send those effects to review before they can affect action
```

## 1. Why This Exists

We do not want every source to be fully verified when it enters the vault.

We also do not want AI output to become formal reviewed knowledge directly.

The review queue solves the middle:

```text
capture stays light
candidate points stay inspectable
promotion candidates become self-contained review records
similar points can cross-check each other
promotion stays reviewed
```

## 2. Queue Directory

Use this structure:

```text
outputs/review/
  candidates/
  collisions/
  clusters/
  archived/
```

v0.1 can create these directories lazily. The contract matters more than the folder existing before first use.

## 3. Core Records

### 3.1 Source Trace

Source trace is the minimum record needed before point extraction.

```yaml
source_id:
source_uri:
source_url_or_path:
raw_location:
source_type: article | paper | repo | docs | video | transcript | screenshot | chat | other
source_family:
upstream_sources: []
version_or_commit:
captured_at:
captured_by:
processing_mode: inbox | immediate
processing_status: raw-only | point-candidate-generated | reviewed | promoted | rejected
topics: []
```

Rules:

```text
source_family marks likely shared origin: same URL, same repo, same author, same org, same article chain, same transcript, same AI output.
upstream_sources records what this source appears to depend on or cite.
source_family and upstream_sources are hints, not final independence judgments.
Source trace records source identity and capture provenance only.
Extraction details belong to candidate points or medium/heavy source records.
Review, promotion, and context-pack use belong to reviewed nodes, promotion records, context packs, or the use ledger.
Provenance is traceability, not truth or confidence.
```

### 3.2 Candidate Point

Candidate point is the atomic review unit, not necessarily an atomic claim.

It should be one small comparable idea, question, risk, decision candidate, preference, or implementation note, not a whole article summary.

```yaml
record_type: candidate-point
point_id:
point_text:
normalized_point:
point_type: claim | interpretation | hypothesis | pattern | risk | preference | decision | open_question | implementation_note
verifiability:
  state: verifiable | partially_verifiable | not_verifiable | unknown
  reason:
review_route: evidence_check | rationale_check | instance_check | risk_check | action_basis_check | personalization_only | research_question | code_anchor_check
why_checkworthy:
evidence_granularity: metadata_only | abstract_or_summary | excerpt_or_passage | table_or_code_anchor | full_source_reviewed
topics: []
entities: []
scope:
  domain:
  project:
  version:
  time_range:
  conditions:
temporal_applicability:
  valid_from:
  valid_until:
  supersedes:
  superseded_by:
  invalidated_by:
  applies_to:
    project:
    version:
    environment:
    timeframe:
    conditions:
  check_state:
  reviewer_note:
source_refs:
  - source_id:
    source_url_or_path:
    source_range:
    quote_or_excerpt:
    source_anchor:
      url_or_path:
      exact_quote:
      prefix:
      suffix:
      captured_at:
      position:
      content_hash:
      archived_url:
      checked_latest_at:
      source_changed: unknown | yes | no
    evidence_support:
      span_supports_target: supported | partial | unsupported | unclear
      evidence_role: direct_support | inferential_support | background_context | counterexample
      reviewer_note:
evidence_packet:
  point:
  evidence_items: []
  grounds:
  warrant:
  qualifier:
  strongest_rebuttal:
  challenge_kind: rebuttal | undercut | scope_limit | source_hygiene | stale_assumption | action_failure
  scope_limit:
  risk_flags: []
  omission_check:
    missing_risks:
    missing_scope_limits:
    possible_omissions:
    omission_questions:
    needs_full_source_read:
  review_question:
  minimal_revision_needed: keep | refine_scope | mark_contested | mark_stale | supersede | reject
extracted_by:
extracted_at:
uncertainty:
possible_related_points: []
review_status: pending | accepted | rejected | needs-edit
promotion_target:

# Optional, high-impact-only review aid.
point_graph_fragment:
  purpose:
  entities:
    - id:
      label:
      type:
      aliases: []
      resolution:
        status:
        note:
  relation_triplets:
    - subject:
      predicate:
      object:
      qualifier:
      scope:
      source_anchor:
      note:
  open_questions:
    - question:
      blocks_review:
```

Default route:

```text
claim -> evidence_check
interpretation -> rationale_check
hypothesis -> research_question
pattern -> instance_check
risk -> risk_check
preference -> personalization_only
decision -> action_basis_check
open_question -> research_question
implementation_note -> code_anchor_check
```

Rules:

```text
If a point cannot be traced to a source range or excerpt, it is not ready for collision.
If a point has broad wording, narrow the scope before promotion.
If a point is an AI inference, mark it as hypothesis unless source text directly supports it.
If a point may be promoted, used for action, used for a strong reviewed relation, or explicitly challenged, prefer an Evidence Packet over a loose source summary.
Point Collision is triggered by candidate / review / promotion / action need, not by raw capture itself.
URL-only source_refs are provenance, not support.
For dynamic data evidence, source identity includes query, filter, subset, as-of, and result-set scope when used for promotion, strong relation review, or action basis.
Evidence support is optional during early candidate capture, but required when a source_ref is used for promotion, strong relation review, or action basis.
Evidence Packet is review input, not a new truth layer.
`warrant` explains why the evidence supports or weakens the point; it is not the same as provenance.
`challenge_kind` names the challenge shape; it does not automatically reject the point.
`minimal_revision_needed` recommends the smallest review action; it does not edit reviewed knowledge.
`omission_check` is required only for heavy source digests, important repositories, long papers, high-impact decisions, or reviewer request.
background_context cannot count as support.
prior chat memory, agent memory, or model background cannot count as current source support unless tied to a current source span.
inferential_support needs reviewer_note before it can count as promotion support.
Not every candidate point can receive truth confidence.
Only verifiable claims can receive truth / evidence confidence.
Unverifiable does not mean useless; it means the point needs a non-truth review route.
Review route defaults from point_type and can be overridden by a reviewer.
point_graph_fragment is optional; use it only for complex, high-impact, cross-document, multi-hop, action-relevant, or ambiguous points.
Extracted triplets are not truth, not support, and not reviewed relations.
If a graph fragment needs more than 3-5 essential triplets, split the candidate point instead.
```

Evidence support is not a Source-node property. It is a source-span-to-target check:

```text
source provenance:
  where this material came from

evidence support:
  whether this source span supports this candidate point, reviewed node, or reviewed relation
```

### 3.2.1 Self-Contained Review Candidate Record

A self-contained review candidate record is required only when a candidate is
prepared for promotion, formal writeback, action-basis use, or a reviewer asks
for a fixed review object.

It is not required for ordinary light capture.

```yaml
record_type: review-candidate
candidate_id:
source_candidate_points: []
promotion_target: claim | pattern | anti-pattern | decision | reviewed_relation | none
proposed_title:
proposed_path:
review_object_state: draft | fixed_for_review | revised_after_review | archived
candidate_body_location:
body_hash:
body_hash_algorithm: sha256_utf8_lf_trim_body_without_heading
evidence_packet_ref:
source_anchor_refs: []
scope_limits: []
risk_flags: []
known_counterevidence: []
missing_evidence: []
approval_integrity:
  approved_by:
  approval_source:
  approved_at:
  llm_assisted: true | false
  checked_specific_evidence: true | false
  checked_body_hash:
archive_reason:
```

Rules:

```text
The record is a review object, not reviewed knowledge.
candidate_body_location points to the exact Markdown section or file proposed for writeback.
Do not embed long Markdown bodies in frontmatter/YAML metadata.
Canonical body hash rule: hash the content pointed to by `candidate_body_location`, excluding the heading, normalizing CRLF to LF, trimming leading/trailing whitespace, encoded as UTF-8 SHA256.
If the candidate body changes after review, body_hash changes and the candidate returns to review.
Approval may approve the fixed candidate body; it must not silently regenerate a different body.
source_anchor_refs must point to independent source anchors, not only to source summaries or the candidate itself.
Evidence Packet remains the support check; the self-contained record only fixes the object being reviewed.
```

Candidate lint before approval:

```yaml
candidate_lint:
  schema_violations: []
  provenance_violations: []
  lint_status: unchecked | pass | warnings | blocked
```

Candidate lint records visible problems before approval. It is review input, not
a rejection verdict, truth change, reviewed relation, or automatic promotion
decision.

### 3.2.2 Review-Gated Compile / Writeback Queue

Any LLM / compile / agent output that proposes a formal `wiki/` node must enter
the review queue before writeback.

This applies to:

```text
source -> generated summary / claim / pattern / anti-pattern / decision
query answer -> proposed reviewed node
brief / proactive synthesis -> proposed reviewed node
agent suggestion -> proposed reviewed node
```

The required path is:

```text
generated body
-> review-candidate record
-> fixed body hash when prepared for review
-> lint / evidence review
-> approval of that exact body
-> formal wiki writeback or archive
```

Rules:

```text
Compile/writeback review is an entrance gate, not a truth layer.
It does not replace Evidence Packet, Evidence Sufficiency Gate, or approval integrity.
It does not create confidence, reviewed relations, action readiness, or automatic promotion.
Approval must write the fixed candidate body; it must not silently regenerate a new body.
Rejected candidates are archived or left auditable; rejection must not delete the source trail.
Raw source capture and ordinary outputs/qa records do not require this gate until they are proposed for formal wiki writeback.
Watch, query-save, MCP write, Obsidian sync, or bulk scripts must not bypass this gate for formal reviewed knowledge.
```

### 3.3 Calibration / Collision Signal

Calibration / collision signal records how a new candidate point may affect local reviewed knowledge or another candidate point.

```yaml
record_type: calibration-signal
signal_id:
from_point:
to_point:
relation: supports | contradicts | refines | risk_of | derived_from | duplicates | near_miss | unrelated
challenge_kind: rebuttal | undercut | scope_limit | source_hygiene | stale_assumption | action_failure
output_kind: candidate_relation | contradiction_candidate | scope_mismatch | stale_assumption_candidate | missing_evidence | synthesis_needed | decision_superseded | review_question | review_due
minimal_revision_needed: keep | refine_scope | mark_contested | mark_stale | supersede | reject
similarity_basis:
  - keyword
  - entity
  - source_overlap
  - semantic_similarity
  - graph_neighbor
  - association_path
  - manual
source_independence: independent | same-origin | derived | unknown
independence_rationale:
relation_rationale:
review_status: pending | accepted | rejected | needs-edit
reviewed_by:
reviewed_at:
```

Rules:

```text
Raw source does not collide directly; candidate points do.
Light capture stays source-only by default; it enters collision only after a candidate point is generated and there is a review, promotion, action, contradiction, or explicit user-processing need.
Similarity only creates a pending signal.
Similarity is not support.
Graph neighbor / association path / PPR-style activation only creates a pending signal.
Association score is not support or confidence.
Challenge kind and minimal revision are review routing metadata, not automatic truth changes.
Only reviewed signals can increase promotion readiness.
Same-origin or derived sources do not count as independent support.
Background citations do not count as support.
Contradictions must stay visible; do not auto-resolve them.
Calibration output is review input, not truth.
```

### 3.3.1 Review Queue Item Taxonomy

Review queue items explain why human attention is needed.

They are not new truth nodes. They are routing labels for candidate points,
clusters, collision signals, decisions, or downstream dependencies.

```yaml
record_type: review-queue-item
queue_item_id:
item_type: low_confidence_claim | contradiction | missing_evidence | synthesis_needed | decision_superseded
target_refs: []
triggered_by:
  - candidate_point
  - calibration_signal
  - point_cluster
  - reviewed_node
  - decision
  - feedback_event
  - action_outcome
reason:
suggested_action: approve | reject | merge | override | request_evidence | mark_review_due | create_missing_point
status: open | in-review | resolved | rejected | parked
reviewed_by:
reviewed_at:
resolution_note:
```

Rules:

```text
low_confidence_claim means the point may be useful but cannot be promoted yet.
contradiction means a challenge must remain visible until reviewed.
missing_evidence means the system needs support for a specific point or relation, not just another URL.
synthesis_needed means several candidates or clusters need a single reviewed judgment, pattern, anti-pattern, or decision.
decision_superseded means a newer decision or evidence package may replace an older decision for some scope.
approve / reject / merge / override are queue resolutions, not automatic truth changes.
override requires rationale and audit trail.
```

Maintenance manifests may add lint-style review items. These can use the same queue, but they remain repair prompts:

```text
missing_intent_confirmation
confidence_cap_violation
validity_window_mismatch
missing_outcome_calibration
orphan_review_candidate
```

### 3.3.2 Approval Integrity

Approval integrity is process evidence, not evidence support.

LLM-assisted labels, model-prefilled review notes, high agreement, majority vote, or a human "looks good" confirmation can create review input or review pressure, but they do not by themselves create evidence review, truth confidence, reviewed relations, or automatic promotion.

For any approval that can affect promotion, formal writeback, benchmark label reuse, or action basis, keep the narrow process facts visible: approved_by, approval_source, approved_at, body_hash, llm_assisted, and whether the human checked the specific evidence rather than only the summary.

### 3.4 Point Cluster

Point cluster groups recurring candidate points.

```yaml
record_type: point-cluster
cluster_id:
canonical_point:
member_points: []
reviewed_supports: []
reviewed_contradictions: []
reviewed_refinements: []
source_families: []
independent_source_count:
primary_source_count:
status: emerging | in-review | promoted | conflicted | stale | rejected
promotion_readiness: none | watch | review | promote-candidate
promotion_target:
gaps: []
warnings: []
last_seen:
next_review:
```

Rules:

```text
A cluster is not truth.
A cluster is a dashboard for promotion review.
Clusters with contradictions can still be valuable, but they promote as conflict-aware claims or anti-patterns.
```

## 4. Minimum Workflow

### Step 1: Capture Source

Write source into `raw/` or source-specific raw folder.

Minimum requirement:

```text
source_url_or_path
raw_location
captured_at
captured_by
processing_status
```

### Step 2: Extract Candidate Points

Codex extracts 2-8 candidate points from the source.

Each point must be small enough to compare.

Good:

```text
Claude-Obsidian uses hot.md as a recent context cache for cross-session recall.
```

Too broad:

```text
Claude-Obsidian is useful.
```

### Step 3: Find Possible Collisions

For each new point, search existing points and promoted nodes.

v0.1 search order:

```text
1. exact entity / keyword search
2. topic and tag search
3. source family / source overlap search
4. optional embedding search later
```

### Step 4: Create Pending Collision Signals

Do not decide truth yet.

Create pending signals:

```text
supports
contradicts
refines
risk_of
derived_from
duplicates
near_miss
unrelated
```

Also record what kind of review input the signal creates:

```text
candidate_relation
contradiction_candidate
scope_mismatch
stale_assumption_candidate
missing_evidence
synthesis_needed
decision_superseded
review_question
review_due
```

### Step 5: Review Collision Signals

Review asks:

```text
Are these points actually saying the same thing?
Are their scopes compatible?
Are the sources independent?
Does one source copy the other?
Is the newer source more authoritative?
Is there a direct contradiction?
Does this signal only show similarity, or does it have evidence and rationale?
Does this association path only explain retrieval, or does it reveal a reviewable relation?
Is this community / theme / graph-neighborhood signal only a retrieval cue, or does it point to a concrete candidate point that can be reviewed?
Is the underlying TextUnit / source anchor available, or are we only looking at a generated briefing?
Does this signal require a local reviewed claim, pattern, or decision to be rechecked?
What supports the point?
What challenges it?
Is the challenge fatal, major, minor, out of scope, or unresolved?
What qualifications limit where it applies?
Is it still valid for the current time, version, project, environment, and action?
Has it been superseded or invalidated by a newer point, decision, source, or observed result?
What risks must be carried forward?
```

### Step 6: Update Cluster

Accepted signals update the point cluster.

Rejected signals stay auditable.

Graph retrieval signals follow the same rule:

```text
community report -> briefing / review cue
graph rank -> retrieval priority
auto-extracted relation -> candidate_relation
TextUnit anchor -> provenance
reviewed evidence + rationale -> possible support
```

### Step 7: Promote

Promotion is allowed only when a cluster is reviewed.

For any claim, pattern, anti-pattern, decision, reviewed relation, or action-basis
promotion, the target must first be represented as a self-contained review
candidate record:

```text
candidate point / cluster
-> fixed candidate body + body_hash
-> Evidence Packet + Evidence Sufficiency Gate
-> reviewer approval of that exact body
-> writeback / archive receipt
```

This gate protects review/writeback alignment. It does not create truth,
confidence, reviewed relations, automatic promotion, or action readiness by
itself.

Promotion examples:

```text
claim:
  two independent sources support a narrow factual point

pattern:
  several sources show the same reusable design

anti-pattern:
  repeated evidence shows a failure mode

decision:
  reviewed pattern + project fit + risk + rollback
```

### Step 7.1: Decision Superseding

When new evidence or a new reviewed decision replaces an older decision,
do not delete the old decision.

Record the replacement link and mark downstream affected knowledge for review:

```yaml
decision_superseding:
  old_decision:
  replacement_decision:
  supersession_reason:
  scope:
    project:
    version:
    environment:
    timeframe:
    conditions:
  affected_claims: []
  affected_patterns: []
  affected_relations: []
  affected_context_packs: []
  mark_review_due: true
  audit_note:
```

Rules:

```text
Old decision remains historical and auditable.
Replacement decision must link back to what it supersedes.
Affected claims, patterns, relations, or context packs are marked review_due when their action basis depended on the old decision.
Superseding changes applicability, not past truth.
If the replacement scope is narrower than the old decision, do not retire the old decision globally.
```

Before promotion, run a lightweight Evidence Sufficiency Gate:

```yaml
evidence_sufficiency:
  state: sufficient | weak | insufficient | out_of_scope | risky
  checked_for: promotion | reviewed_relation | decision | action_basis | risk_sensitive_answer
  missing: []
  required_next_step: proceed | ask_user | retrieve_more | create_missing_point | narrow_scope | mark_not_actionable | needs_review
  reviewer_note:
```

Rules:

```text
Do not run this gate for ordinary capture or ordinary query.
Run it for promotion, strong reviewed relation, decision, context pack / action basis, risk_sensitive_answer, or explicit "is this enough?" questions.
sufficient can proceed.
weak must expose gaps and weak support.
insufficient cannot produce promotion or ready action basis.
out_of_scope needs a scope mismatch note.
out_of_scope may require narrow_scope instead of more retrieval.
risky needs action_readiness: risky or needs_review.
retrieve_more is a recommendation unless the user explicitly authorizes retrieval.
```

Known omission check is review input, not action readiness.

For context-pack, action-basis, risk-sensitive, or explicit "is this enough" reviews, check whether known support, known counterevidence, known gaps, and known conflicts are visible. The result may be enough_for_action_basis_review, not_enough, or conflicting.

This check must not create a global evidence pool, coverage score, automatic retrieve_more action, truth confidence, or ready action basis. Missing evidence or visible conflict creates review pressure, scope narrowing, or a next-step recommendation only.

## 5. Promotion Rules

### Claim Promotion

Minimum:

```text
at least one source-backed candidate point
source range or excerpt
evidence_support marked direct_support, or inferential_support with reviewer_note
evidence_sufficiency not insufficient / out_of_scope for promotion
no unresolved high-severity contradiction
reviewed collision signals if cross-source support is claimed
```

Strong candidate:

```text
2+ independent source families support it
or 1 primary source directly supports it
```

### Pattern Promotion

Minimum:

```text
one or more reviewed claims
one reusable design statement
applicable context
failure condition
negative evidence or explicit "not found yet"
```

### Decision Promotion

Minimum:

```text
reviewed source-backed basis
risk
preconditions
rollback
success metric
feedback path
evidence_sufficiency checked for action_basis or decision
```

## 6. What Must Not Be Automated

v0.1 forbids:

```text
auto cross-checked from similarity
auto support from similarity
auto confidence increase from repetition
counting same-origin copies as independent support
counting AI summaries as independent sources
auto contradiction resolution
auto promotion to claim / pattern / decision
auto creation of reviewed relations from calibration signals
vector score as evidence
graph traversal / PPR / association score as evidence
association neighborhood activation as promotion support
silent overwrite of older claims
```

## 6.1 Evidence-Aware Review Fields

When a candidate point, cluster, claim, pattern, anti-pattern, or decision is reviewed, prefer this compact review panel:

Lazy boundary:

```text
Ordinary candidate capture does not need the full panel.
It only needs a short uncertainty / risk note when relevant.

Expand the full support / challenge / qualification / risk panel only for:
claim / pattern / anti-pattern / decision promotion
context pack / action basis
strong reviewed relation
explicit contradiction
stale or contested claim
reviewer-requested standing check
```

```yaml
review_state: candidate | reviewed | rejected
knowledge_state: standing | contested | stale | superseded | retired
attack_assessment:
  severity: fatal | major | minor | out_of_scope | unresolved
  reason:
  affected_scope:
  requires_review_due: true | false
qualification:
  applies_when:
  does_not_apply_when:
  version_scope:
  project_scope:
  domain_scope:
  time_scope:
  environment_scope:
  assumptions:
review_summary:
  why_it_still_holds:
  strongest_challenge:
  challenge_kind: rebuttal | undercut | scope_limit | source_hygiene | stale_assumption | action_failure
  scope_limits:
  known_risks:
  review_due_when:
    - new_primary_source
    - contradiction
    - correction_or_retraction
    - source_version_change
    - dependency_release_change
    - action_failure
    - missed_feedback
    - stale_assumption
  minimal_revision_needed: keep | refine_scope | mark_contested | mark_stale | supersede | reject
action_readiness:
  state: ready | not_ready | risky | needs_review
  reason:
```

Rules:

```text
reviewed means it was examined.
standing means it currently holds.
contested means it has a material challenge.
stale means time, version, or scope may have moved.
stale is review pressure, not automatic expiry.
valid_until / out_of_scope is an applicability boundary, not historical falsity.
action_readiness is separate from truth status.
not_enough_evidence is a valid review result, not a failure to produce an answer.
action_failure can trigger review_due through the Use Ledger; it does not directly reject the reviewed point.
```

Relations also need state:

```yaml
relation_state: candidate | reviewed | rejected | stale
```

Rules:

```text
candidate relation:
  suggested by calibration

reviewed relation:
  accepted with evidence and rationale

rejected relation:
  looked related, but review says it is not a valid relation

stale relation:
  used to hold, but time, version, or scope changed
```

## 7. Lint Rules

First lint checks:

```text
candidate point without source_refs
candidate point without source_range or excerpt
promotion support from URL-only source_ref
promotion support from evidence_role: background_context
promotion or ready action basis support from prior chat memory, agent memory, or model background without current source span support
promotion support from inferential_support without reviewer_note
promotion support from span_supports_target: unsupported or unclear
dynamic data evidence used for promotion or ready action basis without query/filter/subset/as-of/result-set identity
promotion or ready action basis with evidence_sufficiency: insufficient
ready action basis with evidence_sufficiency: out_of_scope or risky
collision signal still pending but used for promotion
support signal with source_independence: same-origin counted as independent
contradiction signal ignored during promotion
point cluster promoted with no reviewed signals
point cluster promoted while status: conflicted
decision promoted without rollback or feedback path
significant action basis without intent_confirmation or success_criteria
high confidence claim with only abstract/snippet/URL/summary source access
valid_until expired but downstream action basis not marked review_due or out_of_scope
stale/review_due used as if it were proof of invalidity
decision outcome recorded without recommendation, confidence_at_decision, user_choice, or outcome_note
```

## 8. First Field Test

Use 10 real sources.

Expected output:

```text
10 source traces
20-50 candidate points
10-30 collision signals
3-8 point clusters
1 promoted claim or pattern candidate
1 visible contradiction or near_miss
```

Success condition:

```text
The queue makes it easier to see recurring ideas and conflicts than reading the source list manually.
```

Failure condition:

```text
The queue produces noisy clusters that cannot support promotion decisions.
```

If it fails, adjust point granularity and source independence rules before adding embeddings or scoring.

## 9. Formal Operator Convention: Audit Shell v1 / Queue-First Operator Cockpit

Status:

```text
ready_for_formal_operator_only_operation_convention
formal operator-only operation convention
not schema guidance
not truth-layer guidance
not user-facing UI
not automatic evidence / truth authority
recurrence / grouping default-off
```

Current handoff:

```text
00_handoff/2026-06-04-audit-shell-v1-formal-graduation-handoff.md
```

Formal verdict:

```text
outputs/review/2026-06-04-audit-shell-v1-formal-graduation-verdict.md
outputs/review/2026-06-04-audit-shell-v1-formal-adoption-recommendation.md
```

Historical candidate record:

```text
outputs/review/2026-06-04-audit-shell-v1-candidate-operation-convention.md
```

Full alpha lineage now lives inside the main vault:

```text
outputs/review/audit-shell-v1-full-alpha-lineage-2026-06-04/
```

This directory keeps the June 1 through June 4 audit shell alpha materials intact:

```text
full ingest simulation
pressure test
800 independent oracle pilot lineage
queue-first cockpit prototype
Pro review package
final closeout package
intermediate zip snapshots
```

Formal operator rule:

```text
Use queue-first cockpit as the formal operator-only convention for backstage
source / record / evidence triage. Do not treat it as reviewed truth,
schema guidance, truth-layer guidance, user-facing UI, or automatic
evidence / truth authority.
```

Hard boundary:

```text
deferred_review_unless_promoted items must generate a promotion receipt and
move to review_now / blocking / regressed before they can be used as
action-basis, rule/convention basis, conflict handling, explicit challenge
handling, or high-risk answer support.

anchor-strength strip is only a front-face summary. Only selector-grade
jump-back can claim evidence landing. source / claim / context / diagnostic /
history / binary landing cannot be described as evidence support.
```

Graduation evidence:

```text
initial formal pilot: 200 / 200 row_pass=true
high-intensity supplement: 3701 total result rows, 0 failures, 0 hard failures
exact eligible evidence anchors verified: 1453
weak legacy evidence anchors quarantined: 67
source-family adversarial cases verified: 480
first-screen unresolved remains 43 vs direct full audit 152
```

Grouping / merging remains parked:

```text
outputs/review/2026-06-04-audit-shell-v1-grouping-default-off-future-note.md
```

===== END DOCUMENT: architecture/REVIEW_QUEUE.md =====

# FEEDBACK.md v0.1

## AI-readable preamble

Feedback improves future retrieval and context activation.

In v0.2 language, this file is the Use Ledger contract.

Feedback must not directly rewrite truth. It can adjust usefulness, scope fit, and activation ranking. Formal truth changes still require review and promotion.

The goal is to make the vault less likely to repeat bad activation paths without hiding evidence or overfitting to user preference.

Reflexion and Reflective Memory Management support this boundary:

```text
feedback becomes reflection / use memory.
reflection guides later activation and decisions.
reflection does not update model weights or truth records directly.
```

Strong rule:

```text
Positive feedback is weak.
Negative feedback creates review pressure.
Explicit correction creates review_due.
```

## 1. Core Rule

Separate three things:

```text
truth confidence:
  Is the point true or well-supported?

usefulness:
  Did this point help answer this kind of question?

scope fit:
  Does this point apply to this project, time, version, domain, or intent?
```

Feedback can adjust:

```text
usefulness
scope fit
activation ranking
review_due
```

Feedback cannot directly adjust:

```text
truth confidence
verification_status
source reliability
decision outcome
```

Those require review.

Feedback ledger fields should make that boundary explicit:

```yaml
affects:
  - usefulness
  - scope_fit
  - activation_ranking
  - review_due

never_affects_directly:
  - truth_confidence
  - verification_status
  - source_reliability
```

## 2. Feedback Record

Use this record for every meaningful correction:

```yaml
record_type: feedback-event
feedback_id:
created:
created_by:
feedback_source: explicit-user | conversation-derived | system-eval | action-result | decision-outcome
query_id:
session_id:
turn_id:
parent_query_id:
derived_from_turn_id:
question:
query_intent: answer | decision | implementation | research | content | memory
query_scope:
  project:
  domain:
  version:
  time_range:
scope_key:
target_type: point | relation | cluster | claim | pattern | decision | context-pack | answer | route | keyword | channel
target_id:
feedback_type: useful | irrelevant | misleading | wrong_scope | stale | missing | contradicted | incomplete | too_weak | too_broad
event_type: explicit_correction | missing_evidence | scope_mismatch | answer_helpful | contradiction_missed | action_outcome | decision_outcome
signal_type: explicit_positive | same_line_followup | narrower_followup | correction | wrong_scope | missing | topic_shift | none
polarity: positive | negative | neutral
signal_strength: -2 | -1 | 0 | 1 | 2
signal_confidence: 0.0-1.0
severity: low | medium | high
reason:
evidence:
  - source_ref:
    note:
effect:
  activation_delta: -2 | -1 | 0 | 1 | 2
  usefulness_delta: -2 | -1 | 0 | 1 | 2
  scope_fit_delta: -2 | -1 | 0 | 1 | 2
  relation_activation_delta: -1 | 0 | 1
affects:
  usefulness:
  scope_fit:
  activation_ranking:
  review_due:
  future_recommendation:
  risk_calibration:
  option_framing:
never_affects_directly:
  truth_confidence
  verification_status
  source_reliability
decision_outcome:
  decision_ref:
  action_basis_ref:
  recommendation:
  confidence_at_decision:
  user_choice:
  outcome_precondition:
    - recommendation_recorded
    - user_choice_or_explicit_override_recorded
  outcome: succeeded | failed | mixed | unresolved
  outcome_note:
requires_review: true | false
review_status: pending | accepted | rejected
reviewed_by:
reviewed_at:
next_action:
```

Relation feedback rule:

```text
Feedback can change activation of a relation path.
It cannot directly change reviewed relation strength.
```

## 3. Feedback Types

### useful

The activated point or relation helped answer the question.

Allowed effect:

```text
increase usefulness for similar query scope
increase activation ranking for similar query scope
```

Not allowed:

```text
increase truth confidence directly
```

Positive feedback is weak by default.
It may boost usefulness or activation ranking within a similar scope, but it should not persist strongly unless explicit, repeated, or supported by action outcome.

### irrelevant

The point was related by words but not useful for this question.

Allowed effect:

```text
lower activation in this query scope
add near_miss signal
```

### misleading

The point led the answer in a wrong direction.

Allowed effect:

```text
lower activation
require review on relation
add warning to cluster
```

This creates review pressure when the route, relation, or scope caused the error.

### wrong_scope

The point is valid somewhere else but not here.

Allowed effect:

```text
narrow scope
lower scope_fit for this project/domain/version
create review_due when scope metadata is missing or misleading
```

### stale

The point may be outdated.

Allowed effect:

```text
mark review due
lower activation until reviewed
```

Not allowed:

```text
delete or reject automatically
```

### missing

The answer missed an important point.

Allowed effect:

```text
create missing-point candidate
create missing-relation candidate
create missing-evidence candidate when the gap is evidential
```

### contradicted

The answer ignored or mishandled a contradiction.

Allowed effect:

```text
raise contradiction priority
require context pack to include the contradiction next time
create review_due for the answer path or relation that missed it
```

### explicit_correction

The user directly corrected the answer, route, scope, evidence, or conclusion.

Allowed effect:

```text
create review_due
lower activation for the failed route
create missing-point / missing-evidence / scope-mismatch candidate
```

Not allowed:

```text
directly change truth confidence
directly mark a source unreliable
directly reject a reviewed claim without review
```

### action_outcome

An action based on a context pack or decision succeeded, failed, or exposed a new condition.

Allowed effect:

```text
update usefulness and scope fit for future activation
create review_due for stale, risky, or contradicted assumptions
create implementation-note or decision follow-up candidate
```

Not allowed:

```text
turn outcome into truth without evidence-aware review
```

### decision_outcome

A decision recommendation or action-basis recommendation later got an observable result.

Allowed effect:

```text
calibrate future recommendation
adjust risk calibration
improve option framing
adjust scope_fit for similar future decisions
create review_due when the failure exposes stale assumptions or missing evidence
```

Not allowed:

```text
directly change truth confidence
directly change verification_status
directly change source reliability
count one successful action as proof that every underlying claim is true
```

MVP rule:

```text
Record outcomes first.
Record the recommendation and user choice, or explicit user override, before recording a final outcome.
Also record confidence_at_decision, rationale, and outcome_note.
Do not record a final outcome from outcome text alone.
Compute Brier / ECE / override rate only after enough comparable decisions exist.
```

## 4. Conversation-Derived Feedback

Some feedback is explicit:

```text
this was useful
wrong direction
you missed X
not this scope
```

Some feedback is inferred from the next user turn:

```text
the user keeps asking along the same line
the user narrows the question
the user corrects the route
the user points out a missing piece
the user changes topic
```

This is called:

```text
conversation-derived feedback
```

It is weaker than explicit feedback.

### 4.1 Signal meanings

Use conservative meanings:

```text
explicit_positive:
  user directly says the answer or route is useful
  can persist as usefulness / route boost

same_line_followup:
  user continues along the same line
  session-local boost only by default
  do not treat as satisfaction

narrower_followup:
  user asks a more specific version
  create or strengthen route / keyword / channel
  do not permanently boost a point by default

correction:
  user says no, not this, misunderstood, or wrong
  lower route / scope fit for this query shape

wrong_scope:
  user says project, version, time, domain, or intent is wrong
  narrow scope fit

missing:
  user says a key point is missing
  create missing-point or missing-route candidate
  do not punish every activated point

topic_shift:
  user changes topic
  close the previous route
  do not write persistent feedback
```

### 4.2 Persistence rules

Do not persist weak implicit positive signals by default.

```text
same_line_followup:
  session-local only

narrower_followup:
  persistent only as route / keyword candidate

explicit correction:
  can persist as negative route / scope signal

missing:
  review candidate, not truth update
```

Only persist conversation-derived feedback when it is explicit, repeated across sessions, or attached to a high-value query run.

Negative feedback is different:

```text
negative feedback:
  creates review pressure

explicit correction:
  creates review_due

missing evidence:
  creates missing-evidence candidate

contradiction missed:
  requires future context packs to surface that contradiction
```

Review pressure is not truth change.
It is a queueing signal for review, scope narrowing, missing evidence, or relation recheck.

### 4.3 Storage

Use append-only JSONL as the event truth:

```text
outputs/feedback/query-runs/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl
outputs/feedback/events/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl
```

SQLite can be added later as a rebuildable cache:

```text
outputs/feedback/index.sqlite
```

If deleting SQLite loses knowledge, the design is wrong.

Derived SQLite tables can start as:

```text
query_runs(query_id, session_id, intent, scope_key, created)
candidate_exposures(query_id, target_id, rank, shown, included)
feedback_signals(feedback_id, target_id, signal_type, strength, confidence, scope_key)
activation_adjustments(target_id, scope_key, usefulness_adj, scope_fit_adj, updated_at)
```

### 4.4 Weight caps

Use explainable caps, not hidden learning:

```text
effective_delta =
  signal_strength
  * signal_confidence
  * recency_decay
  * exposure_weight
```

Limits:

```text
per_session_cap:
  each target can move at most +/-1 per session

persistent_cap:
  usefulness and scope-fit adjustment stay between -2 and +2

decay:
  old conversation-derived feedback fades unless repeated
```

## 5. Query Run Record

Feedback needs a query run to attach to.

Template:

```text
templates/query-run.md
```

```yaml
record_type: query-run
query_id:
session_id:
turn_id:
parent_query_id:
created:
question_summary:
query_intent:
query_scope:
scope_key:
inferred_keywords: []
seed_points: []
expansion_depth:
activated_points: []
activated_relations: []
activated_routes: []
included_sources: []
excluded_candidates: []
answer_path:
warnings: []
gaps: []
```

This can live under:

```text
outputs/qa/
outputs/review/query-runs/
outputs/feedback/query-runs/
```

v0.1 can store only important query runs, not every small chat.

## 6. Activation Scoring v0.1

Use simple explainable scoring.

```text
activation_score =
  query_relevance
  + relation_strength
  + reviewed_bonus
  + source_independence_bonus
  + usefulness_adjustment
  + scope_fit_adjustment
  + conversation_route_adjustment
  - stale_penalty
  - unresolved_contradiction_penalty
  - wrong_scope_penalty
```

Each part should be explainable in the context pack.

No hidden model-only score should decide what enters the final answer.

## 7. Feedback Safety Rules

Hard rules:

```text
Feedback does not change truth confidence directly.
Feedback does not delete contradictions.
Feedback does not hide negative evidence.
Feedback does not promote candidates.
Feedback does not lower source reliability from one bad answer.
Feedback does not make user preference equal truth.
Conversation-derived feedback does not become persistent by default.
Continuation does not equal satisfaction.
```

Feedback can create review work:

```text
relation needs review
point scope needs narrowing
claim may be stale
missing point should be extracted
context pack should include contradiction
```

Review due should be triggered by concrete signals, not only by dates:

```text
new_primary_source
contradiction
correction_or_retraction
source_version_change
dependency_release_change
action_failure
missed_feedback
stale_assumption
```

Agent memory quarantine:

```text
Future agent / MCP memory writes can create candidate or feedback-event records.
They cannot directly write reviewed truth, change truth confidence, create reviewed relations, or promote knowledge.
```

Feedback polarity rules:

```text
Positive feedback is weak.
Negative feedback creates review pressure.
Explicit correction creates review_due.
```

## 8. L2 / L3 Expansion Rules

Default query expansion:

```text
L1:
  directly matched seed points and reviewed promoted nodes

L2:
  reviewed supports / contradicts / refines / risk_of / derived_from edges

L3:
  disabled by default
```

Allow L3 only when:

```text
the user asks for deep analysis
the edges are reviewed
the query budget allows it
or the system is building a decision context pack
```

This prevents graph explosion.

## 9. First Trial

Run after the first point-collision trial.

Procedure:

```text
1. Ask one real question against the 10-source trial set.
2. Record seed points and activated L2 points.
3. Produce an answer with supports, contradictions, gaps, and warnings.
4. User marks 2-5 activations as useful / irrelevant / wrong_scope / missing.
5. Write feedback-event records.
6. Ask a similar question and check whether activation improves.
7. Add one conversation-derived feedback trial:
   same_line_followup, narrower_followup, correction, wrong_scope, or missing.
```

Success condition:

```text
The second answer includes fewer irrelevant activations and does not hide contradiction.
```

Failure condition:

```text
Feedback makes the answer more agreeable but less evidence-aware.
```

## 10. Current Stop Point

v0.1 defines feedback as a record and ranking input.

It does not implement automatic learning, nightly rewrites, or hidden weight updates.

===== END DOCUMENT: architecture/FEEDBACK.md =====


===== BEGIN DOCUMENT: architecture/00_handoff/2026-06-22-vaks-guard-closeout-and-bases-park-handoff.md =====
# VAKS Guard 收尾 & p2-18 Bases Park — Handoff (2026-06-22)

## 这轮做了什么

VAKS Guard 已正式落地为 VAKS 的只读工具层：

- `tools/vaks_guard.py` + `tools/tests/vaks_guard/`，read-only lazy gate，
  非服务 / 非 hook / 非 Obsidian 插件，不自动运行，不写库。
- commit `75d757af`：新增 VAKS Guard 工具层。
- commit `e5540997`：在 `AGENTS.md` 记录 Agent 按需调用约定。

## 20 个 sandbox slice 的处理

20 个 sandbox 中，**当前主线只收编 7 个 Guard 能力**：

- `body_hash`
- `support_boundary`
- `action_readiness`
- `feedback`
- `failure_matrix`
- `receipt`（检索 + 操作，含 P0-01 / P0-02 收据契约）
- `runtime_artifacts`

其余 slice **保持 sandbox / reference 状态，不自动并入主线**：

- P1-09 查询循环：待查询运行时存在后再议。
- P1-11 语义锚点：待真实 evidence / review 记录与 anchor 形态成熟后再议。
- P1.5-12 到 P1.5-16（RAG / MCP / 解析器 / mdvs / MISA）：运行时边界，parked。
- p2-17 / p2-19 / p2-20（use-surface / 产品筛选 / workflow）：未来产品阶段参考。

这些保留未来价值，但当前不构成主线能力，也不自动合并。

## p2-18 Bases cockpit — PARK_FOR_LATER

p2-18 的两个 Bases 视图设计合理、属 display-only、契合现有 `wiki/bases/` 线，
但当前零真实数据源，故 `PARK_FOR_LATER`（非 `REJECT`）：

- `review-candidates.base`：依赖真实 `review_candidate` 记录（现存真实记录 0）。
- `feedback-events.base`：依赖真实 `feedback_event` 记录（现存真实记录 0）。

现存匹配项来自 VAKS Guard 测试 fixture，不是 vault 知识记录；
`REVIEW_QUEUE.md` / `FEEDBACK.md` 是散文台账，不是逐条结构化记录。

`operator-cockpit.base` / `context-packs.base` 数据更不成熟，继续排除。

### 解锁条件

满足后再轻量采纳，且仅新增 `wiki/bases/*.base`：

- `review-candidates.base`：真实 `review_candidate` 记录落到固定 wiki 文件夹，
  约 5 条以上，且带 `review_status` / `body_hash` / `missing_evidence` / `risk_flags`。
- `feedback-events.base`：真实 `feedback_event` 以独立 Markdown 存在，
  约 5 条以上，且带 `event_kind` / `target_ref` / `review_pressure_effect`。
- 采纳时照 `decisions.base` 套路，filter 按文件夹收口（防抓 fixture）。
  首版采纳应只新增 `wiki/bases/*.base`，不顺手改 `SCHEMA.md` /
  `FUSION_ARCHITECTURE_V0_2_DRAFT.md` / `README.md` / `templates/` / `tools/`。

## 边界

本 handoff 为收尾记录，非架构扩张。主架构、知识流、权威关系均未改变。

没有新增 `.base` 文件，没有修改 Obsidian vault，没有 sync，没有 push。

  resource is real-use mileage and the review->promotion funnel, NOT more design.

## 6. Three-Window Execution Plan (parallel, non-colliding)

Each window owns a separate file territory; they can run simultaneously safely.

- Window 1 — Review->Promotion Funnel (mainline priority)

  - Never write mainline; commit records before cleanup.
- Window 3 — Obsidian Use Surface (safest, zero repo collision)
  - Territory: `H:\Obsidian\资料库\` only.

    by-layer, recent intake); a light MOC to replace the long flat index.
  - Navigation/display only; dashboards != truth; do not touch repo mainline.

Coordination: Windows 1 and 2 share the git repo but different subdirs; each
commits ONLY its own territory, never `git add .`.

Single-window priority: open Window 1. Two windows: 1 + 3. Three: 1 + 2 + 3.

## 8. 2026-06-14 Source Annotation Replacement

- User explicitly asked to replace the old source-level tag/sticky-note idea with
  the Obsidian-footnote-inspired Source Annotation Sidecar design.
- New boundary: inline footnote / highlight is only the human-friendly entrance;
  canonical data is `record_kind: source_annotation` under

# Architecture Review Window Prompt

Use this when opening a new window to continue framework design, reference-library comparison, or architecture collision work.

```text
当前目录：
C:\Users\Administrator\Documents\资料库

本窗口模式：
只读分析 / 架构打磨 / 等我发仓库和资料。
不要执行实现。
不要改文件。
不要 commit。
不要 push。
不要急着做 dev context pack、query-run、feedback-event、PUBLISHING_PIPELINE 或自动化脚本。
先帮我基于新资料继续打磨整体框架。
目标不是把所有好点子都塞进系统，而是各取所长，找到更聪明、更优雅、更舒服的融合设计。
不要把架构变得繁琐、臃肿、制度化。
默认追求：体验轻、判断准、必要时严格、平时不打扰。

先读这些文件建立记忆层：
AGENTS.md
00_handoff/2026-05-28-verified-action-knowledge-system-v0-2-handoff.md
README.md
FUSION_ARCHITECTURE_V0_2_DRAFT.md
SECOND_BRAIN_MODEL.md
ARCHITECTURE_PRINCIPLES.md
SCHEMA.md
IMPLEMENTATION_BLUEPRINT.md
SOURCE_INTAKE.md
REFERENCE_REVIEW_PROTOCOL.md
MEMORY_TIER.md
DEV_CONTEXT.md
REVIEW_QUEUE.md
FEEDBACK.md
wiki/indexes/knowledge-graph-index.md
wiki/indexes/memory-tier-reference-index.md

当前架构你要记住：

1. Karpathy 是日常简单主线：
raw / wiki / index / log / ingest / query / lint。
作用是保持资料库简单、可维护、能被 AI 编译。

2. Dusk 是项目桌面体验：
scope dashboard / project desktop / mailbox / closeout。
作用是做项目时把相关资料、任务、风险、参考摆到桌面上。
它是 workbench，不是 reviewed knowledge layer。

3. Verified Action Knowledge System 是后台可信系统：
source provenance / candidate / review / schema / pattern / anti-pattern / decision / context pack / feedback。
作用是处理强资料、关键决策、开发依据、风险和长期可复用知识。
它只在强资料和重要行动上变严格，不要让普通资料变成负担。

4. SOURCE_INTAKE 的规则：
普通资料 light 处理。
项目资料 medium 处理。
论文、高价值仓库、官方文档、关键架构资料、风险/反模式等 strong sources 才 heavy 处理。

5. REFERENCE_REVIEW_PROTOCOL 的规则：
当前支线可以顺手推进，但每个库必须从全貌架构过一遍。
不要只看冷热分层。
必须同时检查：
- Karpathy spine
- Dusk workbench
- source ingest
- review queue
- schema / reviewed knowledge
- memory / dev context
- feedback
- retrieval / cache
- API / MCP / agent access
- viewer / workbench
- publishing
- risk / anti-pattern / source hygiene

我接下来会继续发仓库、论文、文章或资料。
你每看一个资料，先不要写文件，先给我口头分析：
1. 这个资料主要解决什么问题？
2. 它跟我们当前架构哪个层有关？
3. 对当前支线有没有帮助？
4. 对整个 Verified Action Knowledge System 有没有帮助？
5. 它验证了我们什么？
6. 它推翻或削弱了我们什么？
7. 有什么 alpha 设计？
8. 有什么风险/反模式？
9. 适合直接用、只吸收设计、观察，还是拒绝？
10. 有没有必要后续写入 source record / pattern / anti-pattern / decision？

校准检查：
先把资料拆成少量 candidate points，再和本地 reviewed knowledge 做双向校准。
不要让 raw source 直接撞库。
输出只允许是 review input：
candidate relation、contradiction candidate、scope mismatch、stale assumption、missing evidence、review question、review due。
相似不是支持，冲突不是自动推翻，重复出现不是自动真理。

Review 检查：
Review 不只是看有没有来源，也要看这个判断在支持、反对、范围限制和风险下面还站不站得住。
区分 fatal / major / minor / out_of_scope / unresolved 的挑战。
provenance 不是 support；有引用不等于有证据；支持变多不自动提高 confidence。

最后加一个融合质量检查：
11. 吸收这个点以后，对我们是正向设计吗？为什么？
12. 和原方案相比，我们的融合版哪里更好，哪里更弱？
13. 这是减少负担、提高判断质量，还是增加流程复杂度？
14. 有没有过度设计或让架构臃肿的风险？
15. 最小优雅吸收版是什么？哪些部分明确不吸收？

注意：
先说人话。
不要硬借鉴。
不要只围绕冷热层。
不要被当前支线带偏。
不要为了完整而堆复杂度。
不要让流程变成负担。
不要把每个参考库的优点都硬塞进架构。
优先寻找能减少负担、提升判断质量、保持体验舒服的 alpha 设计。
不要把 dashboard、retrieval、metadata 当 reviewed knowledge。
不要把自动化和脚本提前做。
先做全方面对比和架构判断，等我确认后再落文件。
```

===== END DOCUMENT: architecture/templates/architecture-review-window-prompt.md =====

# Context Pack Manifest

## Boundary

A context pack prepares attention. It is not action basis by itself.

Every item must carry enough boundary metadata for an agent or reviewer to see
why it was included and what it is allowed to do.

## Items

```yaml
    record_kind:

      lexical:
```

# Review Candidate

## Point


## Self-Contained Review Object

Use this block when the candidate is being prepared for promotion, formal
writeback, strong reviewed relation, action basis, or explicit reviewer audit.
Leave it blank for ordinary light capture.

Canonical self-contained review object fields live in top-level frontmatter.
Do not create a second nested schema block here.

Required when `review_object_state` is `fixed_for_review` or later:

- `candidate_id`
- `promotion_target`
- `proposed_title`
- `proposed_path`
- `candidate_body_location`
- `body_hash`
- `body_hash_algorithm`
- `evidence_packet_ref`
- `source_anchor_refs`
- `scope_limits`
- `risk_flags`

Rules:

```text
The self-contained review object is not reviewed knowledge.
It fixes the body being reviewed so approval and writeback refer to the same artifact.
Keep the candidate body in a Markdown section or referenced file, not inside frontmatter/YAML.
If the candidate body changes after review, body_hash changes and review must run again.
Canonical body hash rule: hash the content pointed to by `candidate_body_location`, excluding the heading, normalizing CRLF to LF, trimming leading/trailing whitespace, encoded as UTF-8 SHA256.
Do not use this block as a substitute for Evidence Packet support.
Do not count a source summary, this candidate, or a prior chat as the independent support anchor.
```

## Candidate Lint

Use this block to preserve schema or provenance problems found before approval.
These fields are review input only; they do not reject, approve, promote, change
truth confidence, or create reviewed relations by themselves.

## Candidate Body

Use this section only when `review_object_state` is `fixed_for_review` or later.
This is the exact body proposed for writeback.

## Review Queue Routing

Use this only when the candidate needs explicit queue attention.
Ordinary candidates can leave it blank and rely on `point_type`, `verifiability`, and `review_route`.

```yaml
review_queue_item:
  item_type:
  triggered_by:
  suggested_action:
  status:
```

- Item type:
- Why this needs review:
- Suggested action:
- Target refs:
- Resolution note:

## Source Trace

Keep the frontmatter `source_refs` light by default.
Use this block only when a point needs source-span anchoring, confidence cap review, promotion support, a strong reviewed relation, action basis, or explicit audit.

```yaml
source_anchor:
  url_or_path:
  exact_quote:
  prefix:
  suffix:
  captured_at:
  position:
  content_hash:
  archived_url:
  checked_latest_at:
  source_changed: unknown | yes | no

source_access:
  access_level: full_text | source_span | abstract_only | snippet_only | url_only | summary_only | unknown
  has_direct_quote: true | false
  has_source_range: true | false
  confidence_cap: low | medium | high
  cap_reason:
```


## Evidence Packet

Use this only when the candidate may be promoted, used for action, used for a strong reviewed relation, explicitly challenged, high-impact, stale, contested, or reviewer-requested.

```yaml
evidence_packet:
  point:
  evidence_items:
    - source_anchor:
      evidence_granularity: metadata_only | abstract_or_summary | excerpt_or_passage | table_or_code_anchor | full_source_reviewed
      rationale:
      evidence_support:
        span_supports_target: supported | partial | unsupported | unclear
        evidence_role: direct_support | inferential_support | background_context | counterexample
        reviewer_note:
  grounds:
  warrant:
  qualifier:
  strongest_rebuttal:
  challenge_kind: rebuttal | undercut | scope_limit | source_hygiene | stale_assumption | action_failure
  risk_flags:
  omission_check:
    missing_risks:
    missing_scope_limits:
    possible_omissions:
    omission_questions:
    needs_full_source_read:
  review_question:
  minimal_revision_needed: keep | refine_scope | mark_contested | mark_stale | supersede | reject
```

Rules:

```text
Evidence Packet is review input, not a truth layer.
grounds says what evidence is available.
warrant says why the evidence supports or weakens the point.
challenge_kind names the challenge; it does not automatically reject the point.
minimal_revision_needed is a recommendation for review, not an automatic edit.
omission_check is for heavy source digests, important repos, long papers, high-impact decisions, or reviewer request.
```

## Confidence Cap / Source Access

- Access level: full_text / source_span / abstract_only / snippet_only / url_only / summary_only / unknown
- Has source range:
- Has direct quote:
- Confidence cap:
- Cap reason:

Rule:

```text
No source span / full anchor means no high confidence.
URL-only and summary-only are provenance, not support.
Source range / line range / quote selector is a provenance handle; it becomes
support only after evidence_support checks the specific target.
```

## Evidence Support Check

Required only when this source_ref is used as support for promotion, strong relation review, or action basis.

```yaml
evidence_support:
  span_supports_target: supported | partial | unsupported | unclear
  evidence_role: direct_support | inferential_support | background_context | counterexample
  reviewer_note:

independent_anchor_check:
  anchor_type: raw_source | source_snapshot | source_summary | reviewed_source_review | transcript_segment | code_anchor | quote_selector | other
  claim_note_only: true | false
  self_support_loop: true | false
  result: pass | weak | missing_anchor | self_support_loop | scope_limited | not_ready
  reviewer_note:
```

Rules:

```text
URL-only source_refs are provenance, not support.
Source ranges and quote selectors are handles for returning to the source, not
support by themselves.
background_context cannot count as support.
inferential_support needs reviewer_note before it can count as promotion support.
counterexample should move into challenge_refs or risk_refs.
The reviewed or review-ready claim can be a trace handle, but it cannot be the only support for itself.
Direct support requires an independent evidence/source anchor.
If support is secondary, partial, missing, circular, or narrower than the candidate says, mark weak / missing_anchor / self_support_loop / scope_limited / not_ready instead of upgrading confidence.
```

```text
This fragment is a review aid, not a reviewed relation.

```

## Support / Challenge Review

For ordinary candidates, keep this lightweight: one uncertainty / risk note is enough.

- What challenges it:

```yaml
  reviewer_note:
```

Rules:

```text
insufficient or out_of_scope cannot be promoted as ready support.
```

# 主张 / 结论：

## 主张


## 为什么重要


## 支持证据

Claim drafts may leave `evidence_support` blank.
Fill it only when this evidence item is used for promotion support, reviewed relation, decision, or action basis.

```yaml
evidence:
  - source_id:
    source_range:
    quote_or_excerpt:
    source_access:
      access_level: full_text | source_span | abstract_only | snippet_only | url_only | summary_only | unknown
      has_direct_quote: true | false
      has_source_range: true | false
      confidence_cap: low | medium | high
      cap_reason:
    evidence_support:
      span_supports_target: supported | partial | unsupported | unclear
      evidence_role: direct_support | inferential_support | background_context | counterexample
      reviewer_note:
```

规则：

- 只有 URL 的 source 只是 provenance，不是 support。
- 没有 full text / source span / direct quote 的 claim 不能给 high confidence。
- abstract_only / snippet_only 通常最多 medium，除非 claim 明确只关于摘要或片段本身。
- `background_context` 不能计入 supported。
- `inferential_support` 必须写 `reviewer_note` 才能用于 promotion。
- `counterexample` 应进入反例 / 冲突证据。
- evidence support 不自动提高 confidence。

## Evidence Sufficiency Gate

只在这个 claim 要晋级、进入 action basis、支撑 decision 或作为强 relation evidence 时使用。
Claim drafts may omit `evidence_sufficiency` until promotion, decision, action basis, or strong relation use.

```yaml
evidence_sufficiency:
  state: sufficient | weak | insufficient | out_of_scope | risky
  checked_for: promotion | action_basis | decision | reviewed_relation | risk_sensitive_answer
  missing: []
  required_next_step: proceed | ask_user | retrieve_more | create_missing_point | narrow_scope | mark_not_actionable | needs_review
  reviewer_note:
```

边界：

- 普通候选 claim 不强制填。
- `insufficient` / `out_of_scope` 不能作为 ready action basis。
- `weak` 必须带 gaps。
- `risky` 必须反映到 `action_readiness`。

## Temporal Applicability Check

用于判断这个 claim 在当前时间、版本、项目、环境和条件下是否仍然适用。

```yaml
temporal_applicability:
  valid_from:
  valid_until:
  supersedes:
  superseded_by:
  invalidated_by:
  applies_to:
    project:
    version:
    environment:
    timeframe:
    conditions:
  check_state: in_scope | out_of_scope | stale | superseded | unknown | needs_review
  reviewer_note:
```

边界：

- Truth is source-backed.
- Applicability is time-, version-, and scope-bound.
- `out_of_scope` / `stale` / `superseded` 不等于 false，但不能直接作为当前 ready action basis。
- 不清楚时间窗口时留空或 `unknown`，不要编造。

## 反例 / 冲突证据


## 支持-反对式 Review

完整面板只在 claim 晋级、进入 action basis / decision / context pack、支撑强 relation、被明确挑战、变 stale / contested，或 reviewer 要求 standing check 时展开。

- 为什么它现在还站得住：
- 已知最强反对：
- 反对严重度：fatal / major / minor / out_of_scope / unresolved
- 适用范围：
- 不适用范围：
- 已知风险：
- 什么时候需要复查：
- 当前行动可用性：ready / not_ready / risky / needs_review

边界：

- 有引用不等于有支持。
- derived_from 只是来源，不是 support。
- 支持变多不自动提高 confidence。

## 标签与量化

- evidence_level: primary / secondary / anecdotal / synthetic / unknown
- verification_status: unverified / single-source / cross-checked / field-tested / conflicted / deprecated / rejected
- confidence: 1-5
- impact: 1-5
- relevance: 1-5

## 关系边 / 关联强度

```yaml
relations:
  - target: "[[node-name]]"
    type: supports | contradicts | refines | risk_of | derived_from
    relation_state: candidate | reviewed | rejected | stale
    strength: 1-5
    confidence: low | medium | high
    evidence:
      - source_id: "[[source-or-claim]]"
        source_range:
        quote_or_excerpt:
        evidence_support:
          span_supports_target:
          evidence_role:
          reviewer_note:
    rationale: "One sentence explaining why this edge exists."
    created:
    last_verified:
```

## 当前结论

## 交叉验证

- 相似来源：

# 来源记录：

## 为什么收录


## 来源信息

- 作者：
- 发布方：
- 发布时间：
- 更新时间：
- 原始链接：
- 原始位置：
- 版本 / commit / DOI：
- source_status: active / legacy / deprecated / unknown
- 采集方式：

## Intake Boundary

```yaml
processing_tier: light | medium | heavy
knowledge_status: raw
overlay_state: none
review_pressure: none | review_due | blocked
retrieval_policy:
  policy_kind: source_identity_only
  pack_allowed: drilldown_required
  action_basis_allowed: false
  warning_code: raw_is_provenance_only
promotion_gate: none
action_basis_allowed: false
truth_mutation_allowed: false
```

Source records provide provenance and findability. They must not carry
adopt/reject verdicts, truth scores, project borrowing conclusions, or action
basis.

`source_identity` is stable source identity only. `latest_observed_capture_ref`
is a navigation pointer to the newest known Capture Envelope, not the only
history and not an action basis.

Optional `source_identity.aliases` may include:

```yaml
- kind: provider_object_id
  value: "github.com:repository:R_kgDO..."
- kind: locator
  value: "git+https://github.com/old-owner/old-name"
```

Provider object IDs help detect rename, transfer, or locator rebound when a
provider adapter supplies them. They are not trust, ownership, evidence support,
or review status.

Older records may contain `source_integrity`. Treat it as legacy ingest hygiene
only. New structured captures should use Source Capture Envelope v1 artifacts
instead of adding more meanings to `source_integrity`.

`adapter_ingest` is ingest hygiene only. It records how a source was captured or
converted and what limits are known. It must not create reviewed wiki nodes,
truth confidence, reviewed relations, verification status, or action readiness.

## Medium / Heavy Optional Provenance

```yaml
extraction:
  extracted_at:
  extracted_by:
  model:
  tool:
  source_ranges: []

provenance:
  model: PROV-lite
  activities:
    - type: captured
      at:
      by:
    - type: extracted
      at:
      by:
      tool:
      model:
  agents:
    - id:
      type: human | model | tool
```

说明：

```text
provenance 只记录来源和处理轨迹，不代表这个 source 支持了某个 claim。
claim 是否成立，仍然要进入 candidate / calibration / review。
review、promotion、context-pack use 不放在 Source 主字段里。
```

- 当时版本或阶段：
- 后续更新：
- 是否 legacy / deprecated：
- 为什么这个时间点重要：

## 可选验证字段

These are source hygiene hints only. A Source record does not own truth confidence, and cross-source agreement belongs to review aggregation / coverage rather than the Source node itself.

## 候选关系建议

```yaml
candidate_relation_suggestions:
  - target: "[[claim-or-pattern]]"
    relation_type: derived_from | supports | contradicts | refines | risk_of
    relation_state: candidate
    why_suggested: "One sentence explaining what this source may contribute."
    source_anchor:
    evidence_support:
      span_supports_target:
      evidence_role:
      reviewer_note:
    suggested_at:
```

Source records may suggest candidate relations.
They cannot create reviewed relations, relation strength, or truth confidence.

## Source Annotation Sidecars

If a specific passage, timestamp, PDF region, or screenshot region is worth
pinning, create a separate `source_annotation` record under
`outputs/review/source_annotations/<source-slug>/`.

Do not put inline footnotes, working-copy annotation text, or source-local
development sticky notes into the immutable raw source card.

```yaml
source_annotation_refs:
  - annotation_id:
    path:
    anchor_status:
```

# Project Overlay

## Boundary

This is a project-scoped sidecar.

It does not rewrite the source card, candidate distillation, reviewed note,
decision, or pattern.

It can guide the active project, but it cannot act as reviewed knowledge or
action basis.

## Target

```yaml
target_ref:
target_selector:
target_hint:
source_annotation_refs:
```

`source_annotation_refs` can point to useful source-local locators, but they do
not replace `project_borrow_scope`, `project_non_borrow_scope`, or
`project_reopen_trigger`.

## Project Borrow Scope

```text
For this project, what may be borrowed from the target?
```

## Project Non-Borrow Scope

```text
For project <project_id>, do not currently borrow <X> as <Y> because <reason>.
Reopen if <goal/scope/tooling/evidence> changes.
```

## Reopen Trigger

```text
When should this boundary be reconsidered?
```

## Confirmation

```yaml
confirmation_basis:
last_checked_at:
expires_at:
```

## Forbidden

```text
decision outcome
review verdict
truth confidence
source annotation as project verdict
action basis
universal ban
```

# Query Run

## What Was Asked

- Verbatim input:
- Model interpretation:

## What Was Activated


## What Was Used


## What Was Excluded


## Warnings / Gaps


## Support Boundary

Use this section when the query answer, decision, review note, or action-adjacent
response depends on a claim as support.

```text
claim_refs are trace handles, not proof.
supported does not mean truth.
answer-ready does not mean action-ready.
test / lint / blind review does not mean reviewer approval.
```

Record whether support came from an independent evidence/source anchor:

```yaml
support_checks:
  - claim_ref:
    answer_line:
    independent_anchor:
      anchor_type: raw_source | source_snapshot | source_summary | reviewed_source_review | code_anchor | transcript_segment | quote_selector | other
      url_or_path:
      selector:
      evidence_granularity: metadata_only | abstract_or_summary | excerpt_or_passage | table_or_code_anchor | full_source_reviewed
    claim_note_only: false
    result: supported | weak | missing_anchor | self_support_loop | scope_limited | not_ready
    reviewer_note:
```

If the only support is the claim note restating itself, mark:

```text
self_support_loop
```

If evidence supports a narrower conclusion than the answer draft says, mark:

```text
scope_limited
```

Weak, missing-anchor, self-support, and scope-limited results can still be useful
for orientation, but they cannot be treated as reviewed truth or action basis.

## Follow-Up Signal


## Feedback Boundary

```text
Positive feedback is weak.
Negative feedback creates review pressure.
Explicit correction creates review_due.
Decision outcome improves future recommendation, not truth.
```

# Feedback Event

## Boundary

This event is append-only.

It can affect activation, routing, scope hints, and review pressure in the
recorded context. It cannot directly change truth confidence, reviewed notes,
decisions, source cards, or candidate notes.

## Allowed Event Kinds

```text
useful_orientation
misleading_orientation
scope_mismatch
anchor_broken_or_source_changed
```

## Scope

```text
project_ref:
observed_in_context:
target_ref:
```

Do not generalize this event outside its recorded project/context without later
review.

## Immediate Effect

```text
retrieval_heuristic_change:
review_pressure_effect:
temporary_project_warning:
```

## Review Escalation

Escalate only if repeated scoped negative feedback appears, action-facing use
was misled, an anchor/source needed for action is broken, or a project boundary /
decision / reviewed relation may need to change.
